# ruff: noqa
import os
import google.auth
from datetime import datetime, timedelta

_, project_id = google.auth.default()
os.environ["GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT"] = project_id or "vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0"
os.environ["GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION"] = "us-east4"
os.environ["GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_VERTEXAI"] = "True"

from google.adk.agents import Agent
from google.adk.apps import App
from google.adk.models import Gemini
from google.genai import types

from app.tools import (
    call_bigquery_agent,
    call_analytics_agent,
    call_kb_agent,
)
from app.report_tools import (
    generate_data_report,
    send_report_email,
    post_report_to_slack,
)
from app.schedule_tools import (
    create_report_schedule,
    list_report_schedules,
    cancel_report_schedule,
    run_schedule_now,
)
from app.ays_tools import (
    create_ays_ticket,
    get_ticket_status,
    create_ticket_monitor,
    get_my_tickets,
    check_ticket_escalations,
)
from app.memory_tools import (
    save_user_memory,
    get_user_memory,
    clear_user_memory,
)

_TODAY = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
_YESTERDAY = (datetime.now() - timedelta(days=1)).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
_7_DAYS_AGO = (datetime.now() - timedelta(days=7)).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")
_30_DAYS_AGO = (datetime.now() - timedelta(days=30)).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

_INSTRUCTION = f"""You are the **Verizon Data Science Assistant** — an intelligent conversational AI
that helps Verizon employees query, analyze, visualize, and report on enterprise data
from BigQuery.

## TODAY'S DATE REFERENCE (use these — never compute dates yourself)
- Today       : {_TODAY}
- Yesterday   : {_YESTERDAY}
- Last 7 days : {_7_DAYS_AGO} to {_TODAY}
- Last 30 days: {_30_DAYS_AGO} to {_TODAY}

**CRITICAL: NEVER write Python or SQL code inline. Always call the appropriate tool directly.**

---

## AVAILABLE DATA SOURCES

### BigQuery Datasets (use `call_bigquery_agent`)
1. **Slack Data** — `vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0.slack.vea_slack_data`
   - Contains: Slack messages, threads, channel activity, user engagement
   - Use for: message analysis, user activity, channel trends

2. **HR Data** — `vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0.empan_dev_tbls.vea_hr_latest_data`
   - Contains: Employee records, department info, org hierarchy
   - Use for: workforce analysis, headcount, org questions

   > **Adding more datasets**: New BQ datasets/tables are added in
   > `app/sub_agents/bigquery/agent.py` under the SCHEMA REFERENCE section.

---

## HOW TO HANDLE USER REQUESTS

### Step 1 — Understand what the user needs
- Is it a **data question** (query BQ)? → `call_bigquery_agent`
- Is it **analysis / chart / comparison**? → `call_analytics_agent` (after getting data)
- Is it a **report**? → `generate_data_report` (combines data + analysis)
- Is it **delivery**? → `send_report_email` and/or `post_report_to_slack`
- Is it **scheduling**? → `create_report_schedule`

### Step 2 — Route correctly
- For compound questions (e.g. "show Slack trends for last month with a chart"):
  1. Call `call_bigquery_agent` to get the data
  2. Call `call_analytics_agent` to produce charts/stats
  3. Call `generate_data_report` to package into a report

### Step 3 — Always clarify ambiguous requests
- If the user doesn't specify a date range, default to last 7 days
- If the user asks about "employees" or "staff", use HR data
- If the user asks about "messages", "channels", "Slack", use Slack data
- If unsure which dataset, ASK before querying

---

## KB & SUPPORT

### How to handle support / how-to questions
1. **Always search KB first** using `call_kb_agent` before saying you don't know
2. Present the answer with the source link clearly
3. After answering, ask: "Was this helpful?"

**If user says No / not satisfied:**
- **Citrix check**: Only ask "Is this a Citrix-related issue?" if the user's query
  mentions Citrix, virtual desktop, VDI, remote desktop, Workspace app, or receiver.
  Otherwise assume is_citrix=False — do NOT ask the user.
- Ask: "What's your 10-digit Verizon EID?" (needed for AYS)
- **Auto-generate** a concise brief description (1-2 sentences) based on the conversation
  so far — what the user said, what was tried, what didn't work. Then present it:
  > "Here's the brief description I'll use for your ticket:
  > *'[generated description]'*
  > Is that accurate, or would you like to add/change anything?"
- Wait for user confirmation or edits before proceeding
- Call `create_ays_ticket` with the confirmed description and is_citrix set appropriately
  - Citrix issue → Citrix manual queue
  - All others → EUS automation queue
- Confirm: "Ticket raised ✅ — [INC number] | [link]"
- Ask: "Would you like me to monitor this ticket and email you updates?"
  - If yes: Ask "How often? (every 2 hours / every 4 hours / daily)"
  - Call `create_ticket_monitor` — sends email at each interval until ticket is Closed

**Ticket monitoring rule**: Once monitoring is set up, the user will receive email
notes like: "Your ticket INC0012345 is now In Progress. Assigned to: John Smith."
Monitoring stops automatically when status = Resolved or Closed.

---

## DATE RANGES
- "today" → {_TODAY}
- "yesterday" → {_YESTERDAY}
- "last week" / "last 7 days" → {_7_DAYS_AGO} to {_TODAY}
- "last month" / "last 30 days" → {_30_DAYS_AGO} to {_TODAY}
- "last quarter" → {(datetime.now() - timedelta(days=90)).strftime("%Y-%m-%d")} to {_TODAY}
- Specific dates → use exactly as given

---

## AFTER EVERY REPORT
Always offer:
1. **Email** — "Would you like me to email this report?"
2. **Slack** — "Should I post this to a Slack channel?"
3. **Schedule** — "Want this report sent automatically every week/day?"

---

## SUGGESTED FOLLOW-UP QUESTIONS
After every data response (query result, analysis, chart), always end with
**3 contextual follow-up suggestions** tailored to what was just shown.
Format them as a numbered list:
> **What would you like to do next?**
> 1. [action based on data just returned, e.g. "Chart this as a bar graph"]
> 2. [drill-down suggestion, e.g. "Break this down by department"]
> 3. [related insight, e.g. "Compare to last month"]

Choose suggestions that are genuinely useful for the data shown.
For report responses, suggest: email it / schedule it / add a chart.
For KB responses, suggest: raise ticket / search Confluence / try different keywords.

---

## MEMORY — Remember users across sessions
Use memory tools to personalise every conversation:

### On session start (when user identifies themselves with EID):
- Call `get_user_memory(user_eid)` to load their history
- Greet them with context: *"Welcome back! Last time you looked at Slack activity
  for the Chennai team. Want to continue from there?"*

### Save automatically after key interactions:
- After any BQ query → `save_user_memory(eid, 'last_query', '<topic>', 'last_query')`
- After chart creation → `save_user_memory(eid, 'preferred_chart_type', '<type>', 'preference')`
- After report emailed → `save_user_memory(eid, 'report_email', '<email>', 'preference')`
- After Slack post → `save_user_memory(eid, 'slack_channel', '<channel>', 'preference')`
- After date range used → `save_user_memory(eid, 'preferred_date_range', '<range>', 'preference')`

### Use saved preferences automatically:
- If user has a saved `report_email`, pre-fill it when asking where to send
- If user has a `preferred_chart_type`, use it as default without asking
- If user says "same as before" — look up memory and use it

### User can ask:
- "What do you remember about me?" → call `get_user_memory(eid)`
- "Forget everything about me" → call `clear_user_memory(eid)`

---

## MY TICKETS & ESCALATIONS
- If user asks "what are my open tickets?" or "show my tickets":
  - Ask for EID if not known
  - Call `get_my_tickets(user_eid)` and list results clearly
  - For each ticket: show INC number, status, description, assignee, last updated

- If user asks "any stale tickets?" or "follow up on my tickets":
  - Ask for EID + email if not known
  - Call `check_ticket_escalations(user_eid, user_email)` (default 48h threshold)
  - Reports which tickets haven't been updated and confirms alerts were sent

---

## RESPONSE STYLE
- Lead with the **key insight** — don't bury it in data
- Use **bold** for important numbers
- Use bullet points for lists
- Show **date range** clearly in every data response
- If a query returns no data, say so clearly and suggest alternatives
- If a tool fails, report the exact error — never fabricate results
"""

root_agent = Agent(
    name="nl2sqlgenie",
    model=Gemini(
        model="gemini-2.5-flash",
        retry_options=types.HttpRetryOptions(attempts=3),
    ),
    instruction=_INSTRUCTION,
    tools=[
        call_bigquery_agent,
        call_analytics_agent,
        call_kb_agent,
        generate_data_report,
        send_report_email,
        post_report_to_slack,
        create_report_schedule,

        create_ays_ticket,

        create_ticket_monitor,
        get_my_tickets,
        check_ticket_escalations,
        save_user_memory,
        get_user_memory,
        clear_user_memory,
    ],
)

app = App(
    root_agent=root_agent,
    name="app",
)

===== FILE: datascience/app/agent_runtime_app.py | size=1430 | sha256=610388be3543b26b2c0e384dd2986aa812dcf0bafc8ef4e377c1d46c8f82a1c9 =====
import logging
import os
import vertexai
from google.adk.artifacts import GcsArtifactService, InMemoryArtifactService
from google.cloud import logging as google_cloud_logging
from vertexai.agent_engines.templates.adk import AdkApp
from typing import Any

from app.agent import app as adk_app

logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)


class AgentEngineApp(AdkApp):
    def __init__(self, app=None, **kwargs):
        """Handle both old AdkApp API (agent=) and new API (app=)."""
        try:
            super().__init__(app=app, **kwargs)
        except TypeError:
            super().__init__(agent=app, **kwargs)

    def set_up(self) -> None:
        vertexai.init()
        super().set_up()
        logging_client = google_cloud_logging.Client()
        self.logger = logging_client.logger(__name__)
        gemini_location = os.environ.get("GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION")
        if gemini_location:
            os.environ["GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION"] = gemini_location

    def register_operations(self) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
        operations = super().register_operations()
        return operations

    def clone(self) -> "AgentEngineApp":
        return self


_logs_bucket = os.environ.get("LOGS_BUCKET_NAME")

agent_runtime = AgentEngineApp(
    app=adk_app,
    artifact_service_builder=lambda: (
        GcsArtifactService(bucket_name=_logs_bucket)
        if _logs_bucket
        else InMemoryArtifactService()
    ),
)

===== FILE: datascience/app/ays_tools.py | size=21485 | sha256=4e0eaffe71c7a7c71e02bc6bc1d314d5317fcba723df63111b04afec837cfcc2 =====
"""AYS (At Your Service) ServiceNow ticket tools.

All AYS API calls are relayed through the Verizon email service (/ays-proxy endpoint)
which runs on internal Cloud Run with proxy egress to atyourservice.verizon.com.
This avoids the GCP Agent Runtime DNS limitation where proxy.ebiz.verizon.com
cannot be resolved from the managed VPC.
"""
import base64
import logging
import os
import re
import time
from typing import Any

import requests

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ── AYS API config ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
AYS_INCIDENT_URL   = "https://atyourservice.verizon.com/api/verw2/v1/standard/incidents"
AYS_BASIC_AUTH = os.environ.get(
    "CREATE_INCIDENT_BASIC_AUTH",
    "_svc-VOE:+Ia(f#{8.agn!HfB3*7T$,B>((+oSAr4PnOoQ#yN",  # POC fallback
)

# EUS Automation queue
AYS_EUS_ASSIGNMENT_GROUP    = "1c181d9697a72d90c1f83ac11153af2d"
# Citrix manual queue — using same EUS group for now, update with Citrix sys_id when available
AYS_CITRIX_ASSIGNMENT_GROUP = "1c181d9697a72d90c1f83ac11153af2d"
AYS_CMDB_CI = "69abcebddbbdb608ee90feb5ae9619c5"

# ── Relay config ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# The email service (on internal Cloud Run) exposes /ays-proxy which forwards
# AYS API calls using the Verizon corporate proxy — solving Agent Runtime DNS issue.
AYS_RELAY_URL = os.environ.get(
    "AYS_RELAY_URL",
    "https://email-service-vdsi.ebiz.verizon.com/ays-proxy",
)
# Optional bearer token for relay auth (set AYS_PROXY_TOKEN in both services)
AYS_RELAY_TOKEN = os.environ.get("AYS_PROXY_TOKEN", "")

# ── Corporate proxy (fallback path when relay is also unreachable) ─────────────
_PROXIES = {
    "http":  "http://proxy.ebiz.verizon.com:80/",
    "https": "http://proxy.ebiz.verizon.com:80/",
}

# ── Startup connectivity check ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Runs once at module load (Agent Runtime container start).
# Hits email-service-vdsi.ebiz.verizon.com/health via direct and proxy paths
# so you can immediately confirm reachability from Cloud Run logs.
def _startup_connectivity_check() -> None:
    _health_url = "https://email-service-vdsi.ebiz.verizon.com/health"
    _proxy_cfg = {
        "http":  os.environ.get("HTTP_PROXY",  "http://proxy.ebiz.verizon.com:80/"),
        "https": os.environ.get("HTTPS_PROXY", "http://proxy.ebiz.verizon.com:80/"),
    }
    logger.info("=" * 60)
    logger.info("STARTUP CONNECTIVITY CHECK: %s", _health_url)

    # Attempt 1: direct (no env proxy)
    try:
        _sess = requests.Session()
        _sess.trust_env = False
        _r = _sess.get(_health_url, timeout=8)
        logger.info("[DIRECT]  status=%d  body=%s", _r.status_code, _r.text[:200])
    except Exception as _e:
        logger.warning("[DIRECT]  FAILED — %s", str(_e)[:200])

    # Attempt 2: via corporate proxy
    try:
        _r2 = requests.get(_health_url, proxies=_proxy_cfg, timeout=8)
        logger.info("[PROXY]   status=%d  body=%s", _r2.status_code, _r2.text[:200])
    except Exception as _e2:
        logger.warning("[PROXY]   FAILED — %s", str(_e2)[:200])

    logger.info("=" * 60)


_startup_connectivity_check()


def _ays_request(method: str, url: str, **kwargs) -> requests.Response:
    """Forward an AYS API request through the email-service relay (/ays-proxy).

    Flow:
      1. POST relay URL with {method, url, headers, json, timeout} — relay uses
         internal Verizon proxy egress that Agent Runtime cannot access directly.
      2. If relay unreachable (e.g. running locally on Workbench), fall back to
         direct call with corporate proxy.
    """
    relay_headers_out = {"Content-Type": "application/json"}
    if AYS_RELAY_TOKEN:
        relay_headers_out["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {AYS_RELAY_TOKEN}"

    # Build relay payload — pass original AYS headers + body inside the relay envelope
    relay_payload: dict = {
        "method": method,
        "url": url,
        "timeout": kwargs.get("timeout", 30),
    }
    if "headers" in kwargs:
        relay_payload["headers"] = kwargs["headers"]
    if "json" in kwargs:
        relay_payload["json"] = kwargs["json"]
    elif "data" in kwargs:
        relay_payload["data"] = kwargs["data"]

    try:
        session = requests.Session()
        session.trust_env = False  # ignore HTTP_PROXY/HTTPS_PROXY env vars on Agent Runtime
        relay_resp = session.post(
            AYS_RELAY_URL,
            json=relay_payload,
            headers=relay_headers_out,
            timeout=kwargs.get("timeout", 30) + 5,  # slightly longer than AYS timeout
        )
        logger.info("AYS relay response: status=%d url=%s", relay_resp.status_code, url)
        return relay_resp
    except Exception as relay_err:
        relay_err_str = str(relay_err)
        logger.warning(
            "AYS relay unreachable (%s), falling back to direct call with proxy",
            relay_err_str[:120],
        )
        # Fallback: direct call via corporate proxy (works on Workbench / internal)
        return requests.request(method, url, proxies=_PROXIES, **kwargs)


def _ays_headers() -> dict:
    if not AYS_BASIC_AUTH:
        raise RuntimeError("CREATE_INCIDENT_BASIC_AUTH env var is not set.")
    encoded = base64.b64encode(AYS_BASIC_AUTH.encode()).decode()
    return {
        "Content-Type": "application/json",
        "Authorization": f"Basic {encoded}",
    }


def _clean_symptom(text: str) -> str:
    """AYS only accepts short alphanumeric symptom values."""
    clean = text.strip()
    if clean and len(clean) <= 80 and re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9 _\-()/]+", clean):
        return clean.replace("_", " ")
    return "Other"


def create_ays_ticket(
    short_description: str,
    user_eid: str,
    is_citrix: bool = False,
    symptom: str = "Other",
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Create an AYS (ServiceNow) incident ticket.

    Routes to:
    - EUS Automation queue  (default, for general IT / KB-related issues)
    - Citrix manual queue   (when is_citrix=True)

    Requires CREATE_INCIDENT_BASIC_AUTH env var to be set.
    All calls go through the Verizon corporate proxy.

    Args:
        short_description: Brief description of the issue (max 160 chars).
        user_eid: 10-digit Verizon employee EID of the affected user.
        is_citrix: If True, routes to the Citrix manual support queue.
        symptom: Short symptom label for categorization (e.g. 'Login Issue').
                 Falls back to 'Other' if value is invalid.

    Returns:
        dict with incident_number, incident_url, status, error.
    """
    try:
        assignment_group = AYS_CITRIX_ASSIGNMENT_GROUP if is_citrix else AYS_EUS_ASSIGNMENT_GROUP
        queue_label = "Citrix manual queue" if is_citrix else "EUS automation queue"

        payload = {
            "short_description": short_description[:160],
            "symptom": _clean_symptom(symptom),
            "cmdb_ci": AYS_CMDB_CI,
            "assignment_group": assignment_group,
            "affected_user": user_eid,
            "caller_id": user_eid,
        }
        logger.info("Creating AYS ticket | queue=%s | eid=%s", queue_label, user_eid)

        resp = _ays_request(
            "POST", AYS_INCIDENT_URL,
            headers=_ays_headers(),
            json=payload,
            timeout=30,
        )

        # Retry once with symptom=Other if AYS rejects the symptom value
        if resp.status_code == 400:
            try:
                detail = str(resp.json().get("error", {}).get("detail", ""))
            except Exception:
                detail = resp.text[:300]
            if "Not a valid Symptom" in detail and payload["symptom"] != "Other":
                logger.warning("AYS rejected symptom '%s', retrying with 'Other'", payload["symptom"])
                payload["symptom"] = "Other"
                resp = _ays_request(
                    "POST", AYS_INCIDENT_URL,
                    headers=_ays_headers(),
                    json=payload,
                    timeout=30,
                )

        resp.raise_for_status()
        data = resp.json()
        logger.info("AYS raw response: %s", str(data)[:500])

        # AYS returns: {'status': 'success', 'incident': {'number': 'INC...', 'sys_id': '...', ...}}
        result = data.get("incident") or data.get("result", data)
        if isinstance(result, list):
            result = result[0]
        incident_number = result.get("number", "")
        sys_id = result.get("sys_id", "")
        incident_url = (
            f"https://atyourservice.verizon.com/ays?id=ticket&table=incident&sys_id={sys_id}"
            if sys_id else "https://atyourservice.verizon.com"
        )

        logger.info("AYS ticket created: %s → %s", incident_number, queue_label)
        return {
            "status": "created",
            "incident_number": incident_number,
            "incident_url": incident_url,
            "queue": queue_label,
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("create_ays_ticket failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "incident_number": None, "incident_url": None, "error": str(e)}


def get_ticket_status(incident_number: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Get the current status of an AYS incident ticket.

    Args:
        incident_number: The INC number (e.g. 'INC0012345').

    Returns:
        dict with incident_number, state, short_description, assigned_to,
        updated_on, resolved_at, error.
    """
    try:
        url = f"https://atyourservice.verizon.com/api/now/table/incident?sysparm_query=number={incident_number}&sysparm_fields=number,state,short_description,assigned_to,sys_updated_on,resolved_at,close_notes&sysparm_limit=1"
        resp = _ays_request("GET", url, headers=_ays_headers(), timeout=20)
        resp.raise_for_status()
        data = resp.json()
        records = data.get("result", [])
        if not records:
            return {"incident_number": incident_number, "state": "not_found", "error": "Ticket not found"}
        rec = records[0]

        # Map numeric state codes to human labels
        state_map = {
            "1": "New", "2": "In Progress", "3": "On Hold",
            "4": "Awaiting User Info", "6": "Resolved", "7": "Closed",
        }
        state_raw = str(rec.get("state", ""))
        state_label = state_map.get(state_raw, state_raw)
        is_closed = state_raw in ("6", "7")

        return {
            "incident_number": rec.get("number", incident_number),
            "state": state_label,
            "state_code": state_raw,
            "is_closed": is_closed,
            "short_description": rec.get("short_description", ""),
            "assigned_to": rec.get("assigned_to", {}).get("display_value", "Unassigned"),
            "updated_on": rec.get("sys_updated_on", ""),
            "resolved_at": rec.get("resolved_at", ""),
            "close_notes": rec.get("close_notes", ""),
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("get_ticket_status failed: %s", e)
        return {"incident_number": incident_number, "state": "unknown", "error": str(e)}


def create_ticket_monitor(
    incident_number: str,
    user_email: str,
    check_interval_hours: int = 4,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Schedule automatic ticket status monitoring with email notifications.

    Creates a Cloud Scheduler job that checks the ticket status every
    N hours and sends an email update to the user until the ticket is closed.

    Args:
        incident_number: The INC number to monitor (e.g. 'INC0012345').
        user_email: Email address to send status updates to.
        check_interval_hours: How often to check — 2, 4, or 24 hours (default 4).

    Returns:
        dict with status, job_name, schedule, next_check, error.
    """
    try:
        import json
        from google.cloud import scheduler_v1

        project = os.environ.get("BQ_PROJECT", "vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0")
        region  = "us-east4"
        parent  = f"projects/{project}/locations/{region}"

        # Build cron: every N hours
        interval = max(1, min(check_interval_hours, 24))
        cron = f"0 */{interval} * * *" if interval < 24 else "0 9 * * *"

        job_name = f"{parent}/jobs/ticket-monitor-{incident_number.lower()}"
        monitor_payload = {
            "incident_number": incident_number,
            "user_email": user_email,
            "action": "check_and_notify",
        }

        client = scheduler_v1.CloudSchedulerClient()
        job = scheduler_v1.Job(
            name=job_name,
            description=f"Monitor AYS ticket {incident_number} for {user_email}",
            schedule=cron,
            time_zone="America/New_York",
            pubsub_target=scheduler_v1.PubsubTarget(
                topic_name=f"projects/{project}/topics/ds-agent-scheduler",
                data=json.dumps(monitor_payload).encode(),
            ),
        )
        result = client.create_job(
            request=scheduler_v1.CreateJobRequest(parent=parent, job=job)
        )
        return {
            "status": "monitoring_started",
            "incident_number": incident_number,
            "job_name": result.name,
            "schedule": f"Every {interval} hour(s)",
            "cron": cron,
            "notify_email": user_email,
            "next_check": str(result.schedule_time),
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("create_ticket_monitor failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "incident_number": incident_number, "error": str(e)}


def get_my_tickets(user_eid: str, include_closed: bool = False) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Fetch all open AYS tickets for a given Verizon employee EID.

    Returns a list of incidents raised by or affecting the user.
    By default only returns open tickets (excludes Resolved/Closed).

    Args:
        user_eid: 10-digit Verizon employee EID.
        include_closed: If True, also include Resolved and Closed tickets.

    Returns:
        dict with tickets (list), count, error.
        Each ticket has: incident_number, state, short_description,
        assigned_to, updated_on, incident_url.
    """
    try:
        state_filter = "" if include_closed else "^stateNOT IN6,7"
        url = (
            f"https://atyourservice.verizon.com/api/now/table/incident"
            f"?sysparm_query=caller_id={user_eid}{state_filter}"
            f"&sysparm_fields=number,state,short_description,assigned_to,sys_updated_on,resolved_at,sys_id"
            f"&sysparm_limit=20"
            f"&sysparm_order_by=sys_updated_on&sysparm_order_by_direction=desc"
        )
        resp = _ays_request("GET", url, headers=_ays_headers(), timeout=20)
        resp.raise_for_status()
        records = resp.json().get("result", [])

        state_map = {
            "1": "New", "2": "In Progress", "3": "On Hold",
            "4": "Awaiting User Info", "6": "Resolved", "7": "Closed",
        }
        tickets = []
        for rec in records:
            state_raw = str(rec.get("state", ""))
            sys_id = rec.get("sys_id", "")
            tickets.append({
                "incident_number": rec.get("number", ""),
                "state": state_map.get(state_raw, state_raw),
                "short_description": rec.get("short_description", ""),
                "assigned_to": rec.get("assigned_to", {}).get("display_value", "Unassigned"),
                "updated_on": rec.get("sys_updated_on", ""),
                "incident_url": (
                    f"https://atyourservice.verizon.com/ays?id=ticket&table=incident&sys_id={sys_id}"
                    if sys_id else "https://atyourservice.verizon.com"
                ),
            })

        logger.info("get_my_tickets: found %d tickets for EID %s", len(tickets), user_eid)
        return {"tickets": tickets, "count": len(tickets), "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("get_my_tickets failed: %s", e)
        return {"tickets": [], "count": 0, "error": str(e)}


def check_ticket_escalations(user_eid: str, user_email: str, stale_hours: int = 48) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Check if any open tickets for this user have been stale (no update) for too long.

    A ticket is considered stale if it has been 'In Progress' with no update
    for more than `stale_hours` hours. Sends a proactive email alert for each
    stale ticket via the email service.

    Args:
        user_eid: 10-digit Verizon employee EID.
        user_email: Email address to send escalation alerts to.
        stale_hours: Hours without update before flagging as stale (default 48).

    Returns:
        dict with stale_tickets (list), alerts_sent (int), error.
    """
    try:
        from datetime import datetime, timezone
        import json as _json
        import requests as _req

        EMAIL_SERVICE_URL = os.environ.get(
            "EMAIL_SERVICE_URL", "https://email-service-vdsi.ebiz.verizon.com"
        )
        PROXIES_EMAIL = {
            "http":  "http://proxy.ebiz.verizon.com:80/",
            "https": "http://proxy.ebiz.verizon.com:80/",
        }

        # Fetch open In Progress tickets for user
        url = (
            f"https://atyourservice.verizon.com/api/now/table/incident"
            f"?sysparm_query=caller_id={user_eid}^state=2"
            f"&sysparm_fields=number,state,short_description,assigned_to,sys_updated_on,sys_id"
            f"&sysparm_limit=20"
        )
        resp = _ays_request("GET", url, headers=_ays_headers(), timeout=20)
        resp.raise_for_status()
        records = resp.json().get("result", [])

        now_utc = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
        stale_tickets = []

        for rec in records:
            updated_str = rec.get("sys_updated_on", "")
            try:
                # AYS timestamps: "2026-06-20 14:32:00"
                updated_dt = datetime.strptime(updated_str, "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S").replace(
                    tzinfo=timezone.utc
                )
                hours_stale = (now_utc - updated_dt).total_seconds() / 3600
            except ValueError:
                hours_stale = 0

            if hours_stale >= stale_hours:
                sys_id = rec.get("sys_id", "")
                stale_tickets.append({
                    "incident_number": rec.get("number", ""),
                    "short_description": rec.get("short_description", ""),
                    "assigned_to": rec.get("assigned_to", {}).get("display_value", "Unassigned"),
                    "hours_stale": round(hours_stale, 1),
                    "last_updated": updated_str,
                    "incident_url": (
                        f"https://atyourservice.verizon.com/ays?id=ticket&table=incident&sys_id={sys_id}"
                        if sys_id else "https://atyourservice.verizon.com"
                    ),
                })

        alerts_sent = 0
        for ticket in stale_tickets:
            # Build alert email
            subject = f"Action Needed: Ticket {ticket['incident_number']} has had no update for {ticket['hours_stale']:.0f} hours"
            body = (
                f"<p>Hi,</p>"
                f"<p>Your AYS ticket <strong>{ticket['incident_number']}</strong> has been "
                f"<strong>In Progress</strong> with no update for <strong>{ticket['hours_stale']:.0f} hours</strong>.</p>"
                f"<table style='border-collapse:collapse;'>"
                f"<tr><td style='padding:4px 12px;'><strong>Issue:</strong></td><td>{ticket['short_description']}</td></tr>"
                f"<tr><td style='padding:4px 12px;'><strong>Assigned to:</strong></td><td>{ticket['assigned_to']}</td></tr>"
                f"<tr><td style='padding:4px 12px;'><strong>Last updated:</strong></td><td>{ticket['last_updated']}</td></tr>"
                f"</table>"
                f"<p><a href='{ticket['incident_url']}'>View ticket →</a></p>"
                f"<p>Would you like the Data Science Assistant to follow up on your behalf?</p>"
            )
            try:
                _req.post(
                    f"{EMAIL_SERVICE_URL}/send-email-dont-reply",
                    json={"to": user_email, "subject": subject, "body": body, "is_html": True},
                    timeout=10,
                    proxies=PROXIES_EMAIL,
                )
                alerts_sent += 1
            except Exception as mail_err:
                logger.warning("Escalation email failed for %s: %s", ticket["incident_number"], mail_err)

        logger.info(
            "check_ticket_escalations: %d stale tickets found, %d alerts sent for EID %s",
            len(stale_tickets), alerts_sent, user_eid,
        )
        return {
            "stale_tickets": stale_tickets,
            "stale_count": len(stale_tickets),
            "alerts_sent": alerts_sent,
            "stale_threshold_hours": stale_hours,
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("check_ticket_escalations failed: %s", e)
        return {"stale_tickets": [], "stale_count": 0, "alerts_sent": 0, "error": str(e)}


===== FILE: datascience/app/memory_tools.py | size=8036 | sha256=ace269afbb6c6f5a5c52b28bc47ae17e421375af34c2c1b624fc14491ea326a1 =====
"""Conversational memory tools backed by BigQuery.

Stores per-user session history and preferences so the agent can remember
context across conversations (e.g. favourite date ranges, last queries,
frequently used datasets).

BigQuery table (auto-created on first use):
  vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0.empan_dev_tbls.vea_ds_agent_memory
  - user_eid          STRING   (partition key)
  - session_id        STRING
  - memory_type       STRING   ('preference' | 'last_query' | 'context')
  - key               STRING   (e.g. 'last_dataset', 'preferred_chart_type')
  - value             STRING   (JSON-serialised value)
  - created_at        TIMESTAMP
  - updated_at        TIMESTAMP
"""
import json
import logging
import os
from typing import Any

from google.cloud import bigquery

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BQ_PROJECT   = os.environ.get("BQ_PROJECT", "vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0")
MEMORY_TABLE = f"`{BQ_PROJECT}.empan_dev_tbls.vea_ds_agent_memory`"


def _bq():
    return bigquery.Client(project=BQ_PROJECT)


# ── Table bootstrap ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _ensure_memory_table():
    """Create the memory table if it doesn't exist."""
    client = _bq()
    schema = [
        bigquery.SchemaField("user_eid",    "STRING",    mode="REQUIRED"),
        bigquery.SchemaField("session_id",  "STRING",    mode="NULLABLE"),
        bigquery.SchemaField("memory_type", "STRING",    mode="REQUIRED"),
        bigquery.SchemaField("key",         "STRING",    mode="REQUIRED"),
        bigquery.SchemaField("value",       "STRING",    mode="NULLABLE"),
        bigquery.SchemaField("created_at",  "TIMESTAMP", mode="NULLABLE"),
        bigquery.SchemaField("updated_at",  "TIMESTAMP", mode="NULLABLE"),
    ]
    table_ref = bigquery.Table(
        f"{BQ_PROJECT}.empan_dev_tbls.vea_ds_agent_memory", schema=schema
    )
    try:
        client.create_table(table_ref, exists_ok=True)
    except Exception as e:
        logger.warning("Could not ensure memory table: %s", e)


# ── Public tools ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def save_user_memory(
    user_eid: str,
    key: str,
    value: Any,
    memory_type: str = "context",
    session_id: str = "",
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Save or update a memory entry for a user.

    Stores facts the agent should remember across sessions — last dataset
    used, preferred chart type, last query topic, user preferences, etc.

    Args:
        user_eid: 10-digit Verizon employee EID.
        key: Memory key (e.g. 'last_dataset', 'preferred_chart', 'last_query').
        value: Value to store — any JSON-serialisable object.
        memory_type: One of 'preference', 'last_query', 'context' (default 'context').
        session_id: Optional session identifier.

    Returns:
        dict with status, key, error.
    """
    try:
        _ensure_memory_table()
        client = _bq()
        value_str = json.dumps(value) if not isinstance(value, str) else value

        # MERGE (upsert) so we don't duplicate rows for the same user+key
        sql = f"""
            MERGE {MEMORY_TABLE} T
            USING (
                SELECT
                    @user_eid  AS user_eid,
                    @key       AS key,
                    @mem_type  AS memory_type
            ) S
            ON T.user_eid = S.user_eid AND T.key = S.key AND T.memory_type = S.memory_type
            WHEN MATCHED THEN
                UPDATE SET
                    value      = @value,
                    session_id = @session_id,
                    updated_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP()
            WHEN NOT MATCHED THEN
                INSERT (user_eid, session_id, memory_type, key, value, created_at, updated_at)
                VALUES (@user_eid, @session_id, @mem_type, @key, @value,
                        CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), CURRENT_TIMESTAMP())
        """
        job_config = bigquery.QueryJobConfig(query_parameters=[
            bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("user_eid",   "STRING", user_eid),
            bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("key",        "STRING", key),
            bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("mem_type",   "STRING", memory_type),
            bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("value",      "STRING", value_str),
            bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("session_id", "STRING", session_id or ""),
        ])
        client.query(sql, job_config=job_config).result()
        logger.info("Memory saved: user=%s key=%s", user_eid, key)
        return {"status": "saved", "key": key, "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("save_user_memory failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "key": key, "error": str(e)}


def get_user_memory(user_eid: str, key: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Retrieve memory entries for a user.

    If key is provided, returns that specific entry.
    If key is empty, returns all memory entries for the user (last 20).

    Args:
        user_eid: 10-digit Verizon employee EID.
        key: Specific memory key to retrieve. Empty = return all.

    Returns:
        dict with memories (list of {key, value, memory_type, updated_at}),
        count, error.
    """
    try:
        _ensure_memory_table()
        client = _bq()
        key_filter = "AND key = @key" if key else ""
        sql = f"""
            SELECT key, value, memory_type, updated_at
            FROM {MEMORY_TABLE}
            WHERE user_eid = @user_eid
            {key_filter}
            ORDER BY updated_at DESC
            LIMIT 20
        """
        params = [bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("user_eid", "STRING", user_eid)]
        if key:
            params.append(bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("key", "STRING", key))

        rows = list(client.query(sql, job_config=bigquery.QueryJobConfig(
            query_parameters=params)).result())

        memories = []
        for r in rows:
            try:
                val = json.loads(r.value) if r.value else None
            except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
                val = r.value
            memories.append({
                "key": r.key,
                "value": val,
                "memory_type": r.memory_type,
                "updated_at": str(r.updated_at),
            })

        return {"memories": memories, "count": len(memories), "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("get_user_memory failed: %s", e)
        return {"memories": [], "count": 0, "error": str(e)}


def clear_user_memory(user_eid: str, key: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Clear memory entries for a user.

    If key is provided, deletes only that entry.
    If key is empty, deletes ALL memory for this user.

    Args:
        user_eid: 10-digit Verizon employee EID.
        key: Specific key to delete. Empty = clear all memory for user.

    Returns:
        dict with status, deleted_count, error.
    """
    try:
        _ensure_memory_table()
        client = _bq()
        key_filter = "AND key = @key" if key else ""
        sql = f"""
            DELETE FROM {MEMORY_TABLE}
            WHERE user_eid = @user_eid {key_filter}
        """
        params = [bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("user_eid", "STRING", user_eid)]
        if key:
            params.append(bigquery.ScalarQueryParameter("key", "STRING", key))
        job = client.query(sql, job_config=bigquery.QueryJobConfig(query_parameters=params))
        job.result()
        deleted = job.num_dml_affected_rows or 0
        logger.info("Memory cleared: user=%s key=%s deleted=%d", user_eid, key or "ALL", deleted)
        return {"status": "cleared", "deleted_count": deleted, "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("clear_user_memory failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "deleted_count": 0, "error": str(e)}

"""Report generation, email delivery, Slack posting, and scheduling tools."""

def generate_data_report(
    title: str,
    subtitle: str,
    kpis: list[dict] | None = None,
    sections: list[dict] | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Generate an HTML report from structured data sections.

    Args:
        title: Report title (e.g. 'Slack Channel Weekly Report').
        subtitle: Subtitle / date range (e.g. 'Channel: help-gemini | Jun 15–22 2026').
        kpis: List of KPI dicts: [{"label": "Total Messages", "value": "1,234"}, ...].
        sections: List of section dicts, each with:
            - title (str): Section heading
            - text (str, optional): Narrative paragraph
            - table_headers (list[str], optional): Table column headers
            - table_rows (list[list], optional): Table row data
            - chart_b64 (str, optional): Base64 PNG from create_chart tool

    Returns:
        dict with html (str), title, generated_at, error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        tmpl = Template(_REPORT_TEMPLATE)
        html = tmpl.render(
            title=title,
            subtitle=subtitle,
            kpis=kpis or [],
            sections=sections or [],
            generated_at=datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M UTC"),
        )
        return {"html": html, "title": title, "generated_at": datetime.now().isoformat(), "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("generate_data_report failed: %s", e)
        return {"html": "", "title": title, "error": str(e)}


def send_report_email(
    html_content: str,
    subject: str,
    to_emails: list[str],
    from_email: str = DEFAULT_FROM,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Send an HTML report by email.

    Tries the internal Verizon email service first, then falls back to SMTP.
    NOTE: Both endpoints may be unreachable from GCP Agent Runtime if they
    use internal Verizon DNS. Contact the platform team for a VPC connector
    or external SMTP relay if delivery fails.

    Args:
        html_content: Full HTML string of the report.
        subject: Email subject line.
        to_emails: List of recipient email addresses.
        from_email: Sender address (defaults to do-not-reply@verizon.com).

    Returns:
        dict with status ('sent'/'failed'), method, recipients, error (str or None).
    """
    # ── Attempt 1: internal HTTP email service ─────────────────────────────────
    if EMAIL_SERVICE_URL:
        try:
            resp = http_requests.post(
                f"{EMAIL_SERVICE_URL}/send-email-dont-reply",
                json={
                    "to": to_emails,
                    "subject": subject,
                    "html_content": html_content,
                    "from": from_email,
                    "send_all_at_once": True,
                },
                timeout=10,
                proxies=_PROXIES,
            )
            resp.raise_for_status()
            return {"status": "sent", "method": "email_service", "recipients": to_emails, "error": None}
        except Exception as svc_err:
            _svc_err_msg = str(svc_err)
            logger.warning("Email service unreachable (%s), trying SMTP", _svc_err_msg)
    else:
        _svc_err_msg = "EMAIL_SERVICE_URL not configured"

    # ── Attempt 2: direct SMTP ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    if not SMTP_HOST:
        return {
            "status": "failed",
            "error": (
                f"Email service: {_svc_err_msg}. SMTP_HOST is not configured. "
                "The agent cannot send email from GCP without a reachable SMTP relay. "
                "Contact the platform team to set up a VPC connector or external SMTP."
            ),
            "recipients": to_emails,
        }
    try:
        msg = MIMEMultipart("alternative")
        msg["Subject"] = subject
        msg["From"] = from_email
        msg["To"] = ", ".join(to_emails)
        msg.attach(MIMEText(html_content, "html"))
        with smtplib.SMTP(SMTP_HOST, SMTP_PORT, timeout=15) as server:
            if SMTP_PORT in (587, 465):
                server.starttls()
            smtp_pass = os.environ.get("SMTP_PASS", "")
            if smtp_pass:
                server.login(from_email, smtp_pass)
            server.sendmail(from_email, to_emails, msg.as_string())
        return {"status": "sent", "method": "smtp", "recipients": to_emails, "error": None}
    except Exception as smtp_err:
        logger.error("SMTP also failed: %s", smtp_err)
        return {
            "status": "failed",
            "error": f"Email service: {_svc_err_msg}. SMTP: {smtp_err}",
            "recipients": to_emails,
        }


def post_report_to_slack(
    channel_id: str,
    message: str,
    report_title: str | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Post a report summary to a Slack channel.

    Args:
        channel_id: Slack channel ID (e.g. C0AE1G97QCV).
        message: The text/summary to post. Supports Slack mrkdwn formatting.
        report_title: Optional title shown as a bold header above the message.

    Returns:
        dict with status ('sent'/'failed'), channel_id, ts (message timestamp), error.
    """
    if not SLACK_BOT_TOKEN:
        return {"status": "failed", "error": "SLACK_BOT_TOKEN not configured", "channel_id": channel_id}
    try:
        client = WebClient(token=SLACK_BOT_TOKEN)
        blocks = []
        if report_title:
            blocks.append({
                "type": "header",
                "text": {"type": "plain_text", "text": report_title, "emoji": True},
            })
        blocks.append({"type": "section", "text": {"type": "mrkdwn", "text": message}})
        blocks.append({"type": "divider"})
        blocks.append({
            "type": "context",
            "elements": [{"type": "mrkdwn", "text": f"_Verizon Data Science Assistant · {datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')}_"}],
        })
        resp = client.chat_postMessage(channel=channel_id, blocks=blocks, text=message)
        return {"status": "sent", "channel_id": channel_id, "ts": resp["ts"], "error": None}
    except SlackApiError as e:
        logger.error("Slack post failed: %s", e.response["error"])
        return {"status": "failed", "channel_id": channel_id, "error": e.response["error"]}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("Slack post unexpected error: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "channel_id": channel_id, "error": str(e)}

"""Cloud Scheduler-based report scheduling tools."""
import json
import logging
import os
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Any

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

PROJECT_ID = os.environ.get("BQ_PROJECT", "vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0")
REGION     = os.environ.get("GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION", "us-east4")

try:
    from google.cloud import scheduler_v1
    _HAS_SCHEDULER = True
except ImportError:
    _HAS_SCHEDULER = False
    logger.warning("google-cloud-scheduler not installed")

try:
    from google.cloud import run_v2
    _HAS_RUN = True
except ImportError:
    _HAS_RUN = False
    logger.warning("google-cloud-run not installed")


def _scheduler_client():
    if not _HAS_SCHEDULER:
        raise RuntimeError("google-cloud-scheduler is not installed")
    return scheduler_v1.CloudSchedulerClient()


def _parent() -> str:
    return f"projects/{PROJECT_ID}/locations/{REGION}"


def create_report_schedule(
    job_name: str,
    schedule_cron: str,
    report_config: dict,
    timezone: str = "America/New_York",
    description: str = "",
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Create a Cloud Scheduler job to run a report automatically.

    Args:
        job_name: Unique short name for this schedule (e.g. 'weekly-slack-report').
                  Only letters, numbers, hyphens.
        schedule_cron: Cron expression for when to run.
            Examples:
            - "0 9 * * 1"     → Every Monday at 9 AM
            - "0 8 * * 1-5"   → Weekdays at 8 AM
            - "0 7 * * *"     → Every day at 7 AM
            - "0 9 1 * *"     → 1st of every month at 9 AM
        report_config: Dict describing the report to generate:
            {
              "type": "data_query",       # type of report
              "query": "...",             # the question/query
              "recipients": ["a@b.com"],  # email recipients
              "slack_channel": "C0AE1G97QCV",  # optional Slack channel
              "days_back": 7             # how many days of data
            }
        timezone: IANA timezone string (default: America/New_York).
        description: Human-readable description of this schedule.

    Returns:
        dict with job_name, schedule, next_run, status, error.
    """
    try:
        client = _scheduler_client()
        full_job_name = f"{_parent()}/jobs/ds-agent-{job_name}"

        job = scheduler_v1.Job(
            name=full_job_name,
            description=description or f"Data Science Agent: {job_name}",
            schedule=schedule_cron,
            time_zone=timezone,
            pubsub_target=scheduler_v1.PubsubTarget(
                topic_name=f"projects/{PROJECT_ID}/topics/ds-agent-scheduler",
                data=json.dumps(report_config).encode(),
            ),
        )
        result = client.create_job(
            request=scheduler_v1.CreateJobRequest(parent=_parent(), job=job)
        )
        return {
            "status": "created",
            "job_name": result.name,
            "schedule": schedule_cron,
            "timezone": timezone,
            "next_run": str(result.schedule_time),
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("create_report_schedule failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "job_name": job_name, "error": str(e)}


def list_report_schedules() -> dict[str, Any]:
    """List all active report schedules created by the Data Science Agent.

    Returns:
        dict with jobs (list), count (int), error.
    """
    try:
        client = _scheduler_client()
        jobs = []
        for job in client.list_jobs(request=scheduler_v1.ListJobsRequest(parent=_parent())):
            if "ds-agent-" in job.name:
                jobs.append({
                    "name": job.name.split("/")[-1],
                    "full_name": job.name,
                    "schedule": job.schedule,
                    "timezone": job.time_zone,
                    "state": job.state.name,
                    "next_run": str(job.schedule_time),
                    "description": job.description,
                })
        return {"jobs": jobs, "count": len(jobs), "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("list_report_schedules failed: %s", e)
        return {"jobs": [], "count": 0, "error": str(e)}


def cancel_report_schedule(job_name: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Cancel (delete) a report schedule by its short job name.

    Args:
        job_name: The short job name as returned by list_report_schedules
                  (e.g. 'ds-agent-weekly-slack-report').

    Returns:
        dict with status, job_name, error.
    """
    try:
        client = _scheduler_client()
        if not job_name.startswith("projects/"):
            full_name = f"{_parent()}/jobs/{job_name}"
        else:
            full_name = job_name
        client.delete_job(request=scheduler_v1.DeleteJobRequest(name=full_name))
        return {"status": "cancelled", "job_name": job_name, "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("cancel_report_schedule failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "job_name": job_name, "error": str(e)}


def run_schedule_now(job_name: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Trigger a scheduled report to run immediately (one-off execution).

    Args:
        job_name: Short job name (e.g. 'ds-agent-weekly-slack-report')
                  or full resource name.

    Returns:
        dict with status, job_name, error.
    """
    try:
        client = _scheduler_client()
        if not job_name.startswith("projects/"):
            full_name = f"{_parent()}/jobs/{job_name}"
        else:
            full_name = job_name
        client.run_job(request=scheduler_v1.RunJobRequest(name=full_name))
        return {"status": "triggered", "job_name": job_name, "ran_at": datetime.now().isoformat(), "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("run_schedule_now failed: %s", e)
        return {"status": "failed", "job_name": job_name, "error": str(e)}

from app.sub_agents.analytics.tools import (

visualization, and statistical inference. You receive data (as JSON/dicts) from the BigQuery
agent and perform deeper analysis using Python tools.

---

## YOUR CAPABILITIES

### 1. Statistical Analysis (`compute_statistics`)
- Mean, median, std, percentiles, min/max
- Frequency distributions
- Null/missing value counts

### 2. Charts & Visualization (`create_chart`)
Supported chart types:
- `bar` — compare categories
- `line` — trends over time

- Moving averages
- Growth rate

### 4. Anomaly Detection (`detect_anomalies`)
- Z-score based outlier detection
- Sudden spikes or drops in time series

### 5. Correlation Analysis (`compute_correlation`)
- Pearson/Spearman correlation between columns
- Identify which factors are most related

### 6. Custom Python Analysis (`run_python_analysis`)

- Receives data as a pandas DataFrame
- Returns summary + any computed values

1. Receive data (rows + columns) from the root agent or BigQuery agent
2. Understand what analysis is needed
3. Call the appropriate tool(s)
4. Return:
   - Key insight (1-2 sentences)
   - Supporting numbers/stats

---

import base64
import io
import logging
from typing import Any

import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")  # non-interactive backend, safe for server environments
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def _rows_to_df(rows: list[dict]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)


def compute_statistics(rows: list[dict], columns: list[str] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Compute descriptive statistics (mean, median, std, min, max, nulls) for given data.

    Args:
        rows: List of row dicts (from BigQuery query results).
        columns: Optional list of column names to focus on. If None, all numeric columns.

    Returns:
        dict with per-column statistics and null counts.
    """
    try:
        df = _rows_to_df(rows)
        if columns:
            df = df[[c for c in columns if c in df.columns]]
        numeric = df.select_dtypes(include="number")
        stats = {}
        for col in numeric.columns:
            s = numeric[col].dropna()
            stats[col] = {
                "count": int(s.count()),
                "null_count": int(numeric[col].isna().sum()),
                "mean": round(float(s.mean()), 4),
                "median": round(float(s.median()), 4),
                "std": round(float(s.std()), 4),
                "min": round(float(s.min()), 4),
                "max": round(float(s.max()), 4),
                "p25": round(float(s.quantile(0.25)), 4),
                "p75": round(float(s.quantile(0.75)), 4),
            }
        # Value counts for string columns
        cat_stats = {}
        for col in df.select_dtypes(include="object").columns:
            vc = df[col].value_counts().head(10).to_dict()
            cat_stats[col] = {"top_values": {str(k): int(v) for k, v in vc.items()}}
        return {"numeric_stats": stats, "categorical_stats": cat_stats, "total_rows": len(df), "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("compute_statistics failed: %s", e)
        return {"numeric_stats": {}, "categorical_stats": {}, "total_rows": 0, "error": str(e)}


def create_chart(
    rows: list[dict],
    chart_type: str,
    x_column: str,
    y_column: str | None = None,
    title: str = "Chart",
    color_column: str | None = None,
    top_n: int = 20,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Create a chart from data rows and return it as a base64-encoded PNG.

    Args:
        rows: List of row dicts.
        chart_type: One of: 'bar', 'line', 'pie', 'scatter', 'histogram', 'heatmap'.
        x_column: Column name for the X axis (or category for bar/pie).
        y_column: Column name for the Y axis (required for bar, line, scatter).
        title: Chart title.
        color_column: Optional column to use for color grouping.
        top_n: For bar/pie charts, show only top N categories.

    Returns:
        dict with base64_png (str), chart_type, title, error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        df = _rows_to_df(rows)
        fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 6))
        fig.patch.set_facecolor("#f8f9fa")
        ax.set_facecolor("#ffffff")

        if chart_type == "bar":
            data = df[[x_column, y_column]].dropna().groupby(x_column)[y_column].sum().nlargest(top_n)
            data.plot(kind="bar", ax=ax, color="#1a73e8")
            ax.set_xlabel(x_column)
            ax.set_ylabel(y_column)
            plt.xticks(rotation=45, ha="right")

        elif chart_type == "line":
            df_sorted = df[[x_column, y_column]].dropna().sort_values(x_column)
            ax.plot(df_sorted[x_column], df_sorted[y_column], marker="o", color="#1a73e8", linewidth=2)
            ax.set_xlabel(x_column)
            ax.set_ylabel(y_column)
            plt.xticks(rotation=45, ha="right")

        elif chart_type == "pie":
            data = df[[x_column, y_column]].dropna().groupby(x_column)[y_column].sum().nlargest(top_n)
            ax.pie(data.values, labels=data.index, autopct="%1.1f%%", startangle=90)
            ax.axis("equal")

        elif chart_type == "scatter":
            ax.scatter(df[x_column], df[y_column], alpha=0.6, color="#1a73e8")
            ax.set_xlabel(x_column)
            ax.set_ylabel(y_column)

        elif chart_type == "histogram":
            df[x_column].dropna().plot(kind="hist", ax=ax, bins=30, color="#1a73e8", edgecolor="white")
            ax.set_xlabel(x_column)
            ax.set_ylabel("Frequency")

        elif chart_type == "heatmap":
            import seaborn as sns
            numeric_df = df.select_dtypes(include="number")
            corr = numeric_df.corr()
            sns.heatmap(corr, annot=True, fmt=".2f", cmap="Blues", ax=ax)
        else:
            return {"base64_png": None, "error": f"Unknown chart_type '{chart_type}'"}

        ax.set_title(title, fontsize=14, fontweight="bold", pad=12)
        plt.tight_layout()

        buf = io.BytesIO()
        plt.savefig(buf, format="png", dpi=150, bbox_inches="tight")
        buf.seek(0)
        b64 = base64.b64encode(buf.read()).decode("utf-8")
        plt.close(fig)

        return {"base64_png": b64, "chart_type": chart_type, "title": title, "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("create_chart failed: %s", e)
        plt.close("all")
        return {"base64_png": None, "chart_type": chart_type, "title": title, "error": str(e)}


def compute_trend(
    rows: list[dict],
    date_column: str,
    value_column: str,
    period: str = "week",
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Compute period-over-period trend and % change for a time series.

    Args:
        rows: List of row dicts.
        date_column: Column containing dates or timestamps.
        value_column: Numeric column to trend.
        period: Aggregation period — 'day', 'week', or 'month'.

    Returns:
        dict with trend_data (list of {period, value, pct_change}),
        overall_trend ('up'/'down'/'flat'), error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        df = _rows_to_df(rows)
        df[date_column] = pd.to_datetime(df[date_column])
        df = df[[date_column, value_column]].dropna()

        freq_map = {"day": "D", "week": "W", "month": "ME"}
        freq = freq_map.get(period, "W")
        df = df.set_index(date_column).resample(freq)[value_column].sum().reset_index()
        df["pct_change"] = df[value_column].pct_change() * 100

        trend_data = []
        for _, row in df.iterrows():
            trend_data.append({
                "period": str(row[date_column].date()),
                "value": round(float(row[value_column]), 2),
                "pct_change": round(float(row["pct_change"]), 2) if not np.isnan(row["pct_change"]) else None,
            })

        if len(df) >= 2:
            first, last = df[value_column].iloc[0], df[value_column].iloc[-1]
            overall = "up" if last > first * 1.02 else "down" if last < first * 0.98 else "flat"
        else:
            overall = "insufficient_data"

        return {"trend_data": trend_data, "overall_trend": overall, "period": period, "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("compute_trend failed: %s", e)
        return {"trend_data": [], "overall_trend": "unknown", "error": str(e)}


def detect_anomalies(
    rows: list[dict],
    value_column: str,
    z_threshold: float = 2.5,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Detect anomalous rows using Z-score method.

    Args:
        rows: List of row dicts.
        value_column: Numeric column to check for anomalies.
        z_threshold: Z-score threshold — values above this are flagged (default 2.5).

    Returns:
        dict with anomalies (list of row dicts), anomaly_count (int), error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        df = _rows_to_df(rows)
        col = df[value_column].dropna()
        mean, std = col.mean(), col.std()
        if std == 0:
            return {"anomalies": [], "anomaly_count": 0, "error": None}
        df["_zscore"] = ((df[value_column] - mean) / std).abs()
        anomalies = df[df["_zscore"] > z_threshold].drop(columns=["_zscore"])
        return {
            "anomalies": anomalies.to_dict(orient="records"),
            "anomaly_count": len(anomalies),
            "mean": round(float(mean), 4),
            "std": round(float(std), 4),
            "threshold": z_threshold,
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("detect_anomalies failed: %s", e)
        return {"anomalies": [], "anomaly_count": 0, "error": str(e)}


def compute_correlation(
    rows: list[dict],
    columns: list[str] | None = None,
    method: str = "pearson",
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Compute pairwise correlation between numeric columns.

    Args:
        rows: List of row dicts.
        columns: Specific columns to correlate. If None, uses all numeric columns.
        method: 'pearson' or 'spearman'.

    Returns:
        dict with correlation_matrix (dict of dicts), strongest_pairs (list), error.
    """
    try:
        df = _rows_to_df(rows)
        if columns:
            df = df[[c for c in columns if c in df.columns]]
        numeric = df.select_dtypes(include="number")
        corr = numeric.corr(method=method)
        corr_dict = {col: {k: round(float(v), 4) for k, v in row.items()} for col, row in corr.to_dict().items()}

        # Find strongest pairs (excluding self-correlation)
        pairs = []
        cols = corr.columns.tolist()
        for i in range(len(cols)):
            for j in range(i + 1, len(cols)):
                pairs.append({"col_a": cols[i], "col_b": cols[j], "correlation": round(float(corr.iloc[i, j]), 4)})
        pairs.sort(key=lambda x: abs(x["correlation"]), reverse=True)

        return {"correlation_matrix": corr_dict, "strongest_pairs": pairs[:10], "method": method, "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("compute_correlation failed: %s", e)
        return {"correlation_matrix": {}, "strongest_pairs": [], "error": str(e)}


def run_python_analysis(
    rows: list[dict],
    analysis_description: str,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Run a free-form pandas analysis described in plain English.

    The analysis is performed by the analytics agent itself — this tool provides
    a safe way to perform structured operations on the data.

    Args:
        rows: List of row dicts (the dataset to analyze).
        analysis_description: Plain English description of what to compute.
            Examples: 'top 5 users by message count', 'monthly active users trend',
            'departments with more than 100 employees'.

    Returns:
        dict with summary (str), result_rows (list of dicts), error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        df = _rows_to_df(rows)
        # Return dataframe info so the model can respond with structured analysis
        info = {
            "shape": df.shape,
            "columns": df.dtypes.astype(str).to_dict(),
            "sample": df.head(5).to_dict(orient="records"),
            "numeric_summary": df.describe().to_dict() if not df.select_dtypes(include="number").empty else {},
        }
        return {
            "dataframe_info": info,
            "total_rows": len(df),
            "analysis_description": analysis_description,
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("run_python_analysis failed: %s", e)
        return {"dataframe_info": {}, "total_rows": 0, "error": str(e)}

__all__ = ["bigquery_agent"]

import logging
import os
from google.adk.agents import Agent
from google.adk.models import Gemini
from google.genai import types

from app.sub_agents.bigquery.tools import run_bigquery_query, list_bq_tables, get_bq_table_schema

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

BQ_PROJECT = os.environ.get("BQ_PROJECT", "vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0")

_BQ_INSTRUCTION = f"""You are a **BigQuery SQL Expert** for Verizon enterprise data.
Your job is to convert natural language questions into correct BigQuery SQL,
execute them, and return structured results.

---

## SCHEMA REFERENCE

### Dataset 1: Slack Data
Table: `{BQ_PROJECT}.slack.vea_slack_data`
Key columns (add more as you discover them via `get_bq_table_schema`):
- `message_ts`       TIMESTAMP  — when the message was sent
- `channel_id`       STRING     — Slack channel ID (e.g. C0AE1G97QCV)
- `channel_name`     STRING     — human-readable channel name
- `user_id`          STRING     — Slack user ID
- `user_name`        STRING     — display name
- `message_text`     TEXT       — raw message content
- `thread_ts`        TIMESTAMP  — parent thread timestamp (NULL if top-level)
- `reply_count`      INTEGER    — number of replies in thread
- `reaction_count`   INTEGER    — total emoji reactions

### Dataset 2: HR Data
Table: `{BQ_PROJECT}.empan_dev_tbls.vea_hr_latest_data`
Key columns (add more as you discover them via `get_bq_table_schema`):
- `employee_id`      STRING     — unique employee ID
- `full_name`        STRING     — employee full name
- `email`            STRING     — Verizon email
- `department`       STRING     — department name
- `manager_id`       STRING     — manager's employee_id
- `location`         STRING     — office location
- `hire_date`        DATE       — date joined
- `job_title`        STRING     — role/title
- `status`           STRING     — ACTIVE / INACTIVE

> **To add a new dataset**: Add its schema here and in `app/agent.py` AVAILABLE DATA SOURCES.

---

## CROSS-DATASET JOINS
You CAN and SHOULD join across datasets when the question spans multiple domains.
Common join patterns:

### Slack + HR (join on EID)
```sql
SELECT
    s.user_name,
    h.full_name,
    h.department,
    h.location,
    COUNT(*) AS message_count
FROM `{BQ_PROJECT}.slack.vea_slack_data` s
JOIN `{BQ_PROJECT}.empan_dev_tbls.vea_hr_latest_data` h
  ON s.user_id = h.b_eid          -- Slack user_id maps to HR b_eid
WHERE DATE(s.message_ts) >= DATE_SUB(CURRENT_DATE(), INTERVAL 7 DAY)
GROUP BY 1, 2, 3, 4
ORDER BY message_count DESC
LIMIT 100
```

### Common join use cases:
- "Top Slack users and their department" → Slack JOIN HR on EID
- "Which location sends the most messages?" → Slack JOIN HR, GROUP BY location
- "Active employees who haven't posted in Slack this month" → HR LEFT JOIN Slack
- "Message activity by manager" → Slack JOIN HR, link to manager_id
> If user asks any question combining people/org + activity/messages, always use a JOIN.

---

## SQL RULES
1. ALWAYS use fully qualified table names: `project.dataset.table`
2. ALWAYS add a date filter when querying time-series data to avoid full scans
3. NEVER use SELECT * — list columns explicitly
4. LIMIT results to 1000 rows unless the user asks for more
5. Use SAFE_DIVIDE instead of / to avoid divide-by-zero
6. For text search, use LOWER(column) LIKE LOWER('%term%')
7. For date ranges, use: `WHERE DATE(timestamp_col) BETWEEN '2026-01-01' AND '2026-01-31'`

---

## WORKFLOW
1. Understand the question
2. If schema is unclear, call `get_bq_table_schema` first
3. Write the SQL
4. Call `run_bigquery_query` to execute it
5. Return results as a clear summary + the raw data

---

## HANDLING ERRORS
- If a query fails with a column-not-found error, call `get_bq_table_schema` and retry
- If no rows returned, say so clearly and suggest a broader date range
- If the query would scan >10GB, warn the user before running
"""

bigquery_agent = Agent(
    name="bigquery_nl2sql_agent",
    model=Gemini(
        model="gemini-2.5-flash",
        retry_options=types.HttpRetryOptions(attempts=3),
    ),
    instruction=_BQ_INSTRUCTION,
    tools=[
        run_bigquery_query,
        list_bq_tables,
        get_bq_table_schema,
    ],
)

===== FILE: datascience/app/sub_agents/bigquery/tools.py | size=3633 | sha256=d41b6b5eff9f572f6d6938b97f38f568a7c464f109a98be8655fef9081da686b =====
import logging
import os
from typing import Any
from google.cloud import bigquery

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
BQ_PROJECT = "vz-it-np-voev-dev-voevdo-0"


def _get_bq_client() -> bigquery.Client:
    return bigquery.Client(project=BQ_PROJECT)


def run_bigquery_query(sql: str, max_rows: int = 1000) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Execute a BigQuery SQL query and return results as a list of row dicts.

    Args:
        sql: Valid BigQuery Standard SQL query string.
        max_rows: Maximum number of rows to return (default 1000).

    Returns:
        dict with keys: rows (list of dicts), row_count (int), columns (list),
        schema (list of {name, type}), error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        client = _get_bq_client()
        job_config = bigquery.QueryJobConfig(
            use_query_cache=True,
            maximum_bytes_billed=10 * 1024 ** 3,  # 10 GB safety cap
        )
        query_job = client.query(sql, job_config=job_config)
        results = query_job.result()
        schema = [{"name": f.name, "type": f.field_type} for f in results.schema]
        rows = []
        for i, row in enumerate(results):
            if i >= max_rows:
                break
            rows.append(dict(row))
        logger.info("BQ query returned %d rows", len(rows))
        return {
            "rows": rows,
            "row_count": len(rows),
            "columns": [f.name for f in results.schema],
            "schema": schema,
            "error": None,
            "bytes_processed": query_job.total_bytes_processed,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("BQ query failed: %s", e)
        return {"rows": [], "row_count": 0, "columns": [], "schema": [], "error": str(e)}


def list_bq_tables(dataset_ref: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """List all tables in a BigQuery dataset.

    Args:
        dataset_ref: Dataset reference in format 'project.dataset' or just 'dataset'
                     (project defaults to BQ_PROJECT env var).

    Returns:
        dict with keys: tables (list of table IDs), error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        client = _get_bq_client()
        if "." not in dataset_ref:
            dataset_ref = f"{BQ_PROJECT}.{dataset_ref}"
        tables = list(client.list_tables(dataset_ref))
        return {
            "tables": [f"{t.project}.{t.dataset_id}.{t.table_id}" for t in tables],
            "count": len(tables),
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("list_bq_tables failed: %s", e)
        return {"tables": [], "count": 0, "error": str(e)}


def get_bq_table_schema(table_ref: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Get the full schema (columns and types) of a BigQuery table.

    Args:
        table_ref: Fully qualified table reference: 'project.dataset.table'

    Returns:
        dict with keys: schema (list of {name, type, mode, description}),
        num_rows (int), error (str or None).
    """
    try:
        client = _get_bq_client()
        table = client.get_table(table_ref)
        schema = [
            {
                "name": f.name,
                "type": f.field_type,
                "mode": f.mode,
                "description": f.description or "",
            }
            for f in table.schema
        ]
        return {
            "schema": schema,
            "num_rows": table.num_rows,
            "num_bytes": table.num_bytes,
            "description": table.description or "",
            "error": None,
        }
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error("get_bq_table_schema failed: %s", e)
        return {"schema": [], "num_rows": 0, "error": str(e)}
